Immersed in a high-stress inpatient environment and often running on coffee and little rest, he began to feel increasingly detached from his art and social life, and his coworkers noticed he was becoming more withdrawn and had trouble keeping up with routine tasks that had once been second nature. Over the following months, his performance reviews flagged missed details in charting and odd comments during team meetings, and he stopped participating in neighborhood mural projects and weekend hikes that previously helped him decompress. When a supervisor, concerned about his marked change and difficulty staying grounded at work, pulled him aside after he requested a schedule change, Nestor disclosed his growing distress about his thoughts and mood, leading to an urgent referral to the hospital’s psychiatric clinic where his symptoms were recognized as part of a schizoaffective picture.\n- Chief Complaint: “Lately I feel this heavy, empty mood all the time, like I’m weighed down and can’t shake it no matter what I do.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep thinking the cameras on the unit and even the mirrors at home are actually there to watch me specifically, like the hospital is running some kind of experiment on my mind and everyone’s in on it, no matter how many times people tell me that’s not true.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I’m trying to talk in report or even just explain something to my mom, my thoughts jump all over the place and I’ll start on how a patient did overnight, then suddenly I’m talking about murals and how the colors are spying on me, and I can see people look confused because I can’t stick to one line of thought.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): Sometimes on the unit or even at home I just freeze up and stand in one spot staring at a wall or the floor for long stretches, and if someone tells me to sit or move I either don’t respond at all or I’ll start pacing back and forth really fast, rubbing my hands together like I can’t control my body.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days it feels like there’s this heavy, gray weight sitting on my chest and inside my head, like everything’s pointless and I’m just moving through mud, and even when something good happens—like my brother getting a promotion—I still feel completely empty and sort of dead inside.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to live for painting murals and playing weekend soccer with my friends, but now even when they invite me or bring by sketches, I just stare at them and say no because I can’t feel any excitement or care about any of it anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): Over the past year my appetite’s pretty much disappeared—on my breaks I just push the food around on the plate or skip eating altogether, and my pants hang off me now because I’ve dropped so much weight without even trying.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): People at work and at home keep telling me I move and talk like I’m in slow motion, like when they ask me a simple question it takes me forever to answer and even just getting up from the couch or walking down the hall feels like I’m dragging a hundred-pound backpack.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately even basic stuff like filling out chart notes or deciding what to cook completely overwhelms me, because my head feels foggy and I lose track of what I’m doing mid-sentence, so I just sit there staring at the screen or the fridge, unable to make up my mind.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): Pretty often, especially after a long night shift when that emptiness hits hardest, I catch myself thinking it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up or if I stepped in front of a train on my way home, and I’ve even looked up which bridge is the highest in the city, even though I haven’t actually gone there.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: On my worst days this past year, I’ll be dragging myself out of bed feeling totally empty and hopeless while also being convinced the hospital cameras and mirrors are tracking my thoughts, so the dark mood and the paranoid stuff seem to hit me at the same time.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Even on weeks when the ideas about being watched back off a bit, that heavy, numb depression has pretty much never let up since all the extra shifts started, so feeling low has been there more often than not.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a stretch a few months ago, maybe a couple of weeks or more, when my mood felt kind of flat but not especially sad, yet I was still totally convinced the unit cameras and the mirrors at home were part of a setup to read and record my thoughts.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve gone from being one of the reliable aides to getting written up for mistakes, calling out of shifts, barely seeing my friends, and letting my room, laundry, and even basic stuff like cooking for myself slide.\n- duration: All of this really started to snowball not long after I picked up those extra night shifts, so it’s been going on for around a year and a half now.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The belief that the cameras and mirrors are targeting me has been hanging around in one form or another for about a year, pretty soon after the other problems at work began.\n- depressive_episode_duration: That heavy, empty feeling and losing interest in my art and soccer have been there most days for a little over a year now, starting a bit after I changed my work Immersed in a high-stress inpatient environment and often running on coffee and little rest, he began to feel increasingly detached from his art and social life, and his coworkers noticed he was becoming more withdrawn and had trouble keeping up with routine tasks that had once been second nature. Over the following months, his performance reviews flagged missed details in charting and odd comments during team meetings, and he stopped participating in neighborhood mural projects and weekend hikes that previously helped him decompress. When a supervisor, concerned about his marked change and difficulty staying grounded at work, pulled him aside after he requested a schedule change, Nestor disclosed his growing distress about his thoughts and mood, leading to an urgent referral to the hospital’s psychiatric clinic where his symptoms were recognized as part of a schizoaffective picture.\n- Chief Complaint: “Lately I feel this heavy, empty mood all the time, like I’m weighed down and can’t shake it no matter what I do.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep thinking the cameras on the unit and even the mirrors at home are actually there to watch me specifically, like the hospital is running some kind of experiment on my mind and everyone’s in on it, no matter how many times people tell me that’s not true.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I’m trying to talk in report or even just explain something to my mom, my thoughts jump all over the place and I’ll start on how a patient did overnight, then suddenly I’m talking about murals and how the colors are spying on me, and I can see people look confused because I can’t stick to one line of thought.\n- S022 I either don’t respond at all or I’ll start pacing back and forth really fast, rubbing my hands together like I can’t control my body.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days it feels like there’s this heavy, gray weight sitting on my chest and inside my head, like everything’s pointless and I’m just moving through mud, and even when something good happens—like my brother getting a promotion—I still feel completely empty and sort of dead inside.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to live for painting murals and playing weekend soccer with my friends, but now even when they invite me or bring by up from the couch or walking down the hall feels like I’m dragging a hundred-pound backpack.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately even basic stuff like filling out chart notes or deciding what to It only started about a year and a half ago, shortly after I began taking on those extra night shifts at work. I used to love painting murals and playing soccer on the weekends, but now I just don't care about any of it anymore. Even when my friends bring by sketches or try to invite me out, I just tell them no because I can't feel any excitement at all.\n\nDoctor: Since your schedule changed, have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns or your daily energy levels?\n\nPatient: My sleep has been completely messed up and irregular ever since I started those late-night shifts. My energy is totally gone, and most days I feel like I'm moving in slow motion, like I'm dragging a hundred-pound backpack just to get around.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you also been struggling with feelings of worthlessness or blaming yourself excessively for things?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been feeling worthless or blaming myself for things. It's more that I just feel completely empty and heavy all the time, like there's just nothing inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder than usual to concentrate, focus on your work, or make everyday decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, my head feels incredibly foggy lately and I often lose track of what I'm doing mid-sentence. Even basic things like filling out my chart notes at work or deciding what to cook completely overwhelm me. I often just end up staring blankly at the computer screen or the fridge, unable to make a choice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death, or felt like life is not worth living, during this past year?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty often, especially after a long night shift when the emptiness hits hardest, I think it'd be easier if I didn't wake up or if I stepped in front of a train on my way home. I've even looked up which bridge is the highest in the city, though I haven't actually gone there or tried to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Before this heavy mood started a year and a half ago, did you ever experience milder, ongoing periods of feeling down or low energy that lasted for several years?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having any long-term periods of feeling down or low on energy before this all started a year and a half ago. My mood and energy were generally fine before I took on those extra work shifts.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of a few days or more where you felt the exact opposite of this—extremely energetic, hyperactive, or like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never had any periods where I felt extremely energetic or hyperactive like that. I have also never experienced times where I felt like I didn't need any sleep."
  }

I often just end up staring blankly at the computer screen or the fridge, unable to make a choice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death, or felt like life is not worth living, during this past year?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty often, especially after a long night shift when the emptiness hits hardest, I think it'd be easier if I didn't wake up or if I stepped in front of a train on my way home. I've even looked up which bridge is the highest in the city, though I haven't actually gone there or tried to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Before this heavy mood started a year and a half ago, did you ever experience milder, ongoing periods of feeling down or low energy that lasted for several years?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having any long-term periods of feeling down or low on energy before this all started a year and a half ago. My mood and energy were generally fine before I took on those extra work shifts.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of a few days or more where you felt the exact opposite of this—extremely energetic, hyperactive, or like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never had any periods where I felt extremely energetic or hyperactive like that. I have also never experienced times where I felt like I didn't need any sleep.\n\nDoctor: During this difficult time, have you heard voices or seen things that others couldn't, or held any strong beliefs that others told you weren't real?\n\nPatient: I haven't heard any voices, but I do have a strong belief that everyone else tells me is not true. I'm convinced the cameras on the hospital unit and even the mirrors at my house are watching me specifically as part of an experiment on my mind. No matter how many times people tell me it's not real, I can't shake the feeling that they are tracking my thoughts."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nNo, I wouldn't say I've been feeling worthless or blaming myself for things.
